People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

inappropriate to the situation.\n  manifestation: There have been times on client visits where I’d stop what I was supposed to be doing and start lining up gloves, pill cups, and crochet scraps in mood.\n  manifestation: I go from feeling so bright and chosen that Greenville can’t hold all my plans to snapping hard at someone over a small thing, like a client asking me to repeat myself.\n- key: S034\n  name: Increased_Activity\n  description: Abnormally and persistently increased goal-directed activity or energy.\n  manifestation: I get so much energy that I’ll clean my whole kitchen, start three paintings, crochet half a blanket, and rewrite care schedules all in the same night without being able to sit down.\n- key: S035\n  name: Inflated_Self_Esteem\n  description: Inflated painting or making calls.\n- key: S038\n  name: Racing_Thoughts\n  description: Flight of ideas or subjective experience that thoughts are racing.\n  manifestation: My thoughts run so fast that one Like I’ll clean my whole kitchen, start three different paintings, crochet half a blanket, and then start rewriting care schedules all in the same night. It feels productive at the time, like I’m finally getting things done… but I guess it is a lot. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: During the times you heard voices or felt people were watching you, did those experiences seem to match how you were feeling emotionally at the time—like if you were feeling down or especially irritable?\n\nPatient: I think… yes, some of it did line up with when I was more keyed up, if that’s the right way to say it.\n\nLike when I was only sleeping a few hours and really pushing these big art and care plans, that’s when I was also hearing the voices in the studio and feeling sure people were watching my work. I wasn’t trying to be dramatic about it — it just felt connected at the time, like everything was moving fast and important.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced hearing voices or feeling like people were watching you when you weren’t feeling particularly down, overly excited, or emotionally elevated?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it was, but there was a stretch after my energy had come down and I was sleeping more normal again where it didn’t fully stop.\n\nThe voices were still there sometimes, and I still had this feeling that people were judging my work or keeping track of what I was doing. It went on for several weeks, I guess. I wasn’t feeling especially up or really down then… at least not in the way I had been before. Does that answer it right?\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a time when you felt completely emotionally stable—neither especially up nor down—where you still experienced hearing voices or feeling like people were watching or judging your work?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that did happen. After my energy kind of dropped back down and I was sleeping more normally, I wasn’t feeling especially high or low, at least not that I could tell.\n\nBut the voices didn’t completely stop, and I still had that feeling like people were judging my work or keeping track of what I was doing. It kept going for several weeks. I know that probably sounds strange… I’m just trying to answer it the right way.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt down most of the day, lost interest in things you usually enjoy, or experienced other symptoms like changes in appetite or trouble concentrating, separate from the times you were feeling very energetic or having these experiences with voices and feeling watched?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can separate it out that clearly. I mean, I’ve had days where I felt tired or kind of low, but I don’t know if it was most of the day or anything like that.\n\nI don’t want to say yes if I’m not remembering it right. I haven’t really paid close attention to appetite or concentration separate from the other stuff. Sorry, is that okay?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period lasting at least two weeks where you felt down most of the day, nearly every day, and lost interest in things you usually enjoy, even when you weren’t having the experiences with voices or feeling watched?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I can honestly say that, no. I’ve had times where I felt low or didn’t feel like doing much, but I’m not sure it was two weeks straight, most of the day, nearly every day… especially separate from everything else.\n\nI don’t want to give you the wrong answer just to be agreeable. I just haven’t tracked it that clearly. Is it okay if I say I’m not sure?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when you felt down or depressed for most of the day, nearly every day for at least two weeks, even when you weren’t hearing voices, feeling watched, or experiencing those high-energy periods?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t think I can say yes to that clearly. I’ve felt down before, but the “most of the day, nearly every day for two weeks” part—I’m not sure.\n\nAnd separating it from the voices or feeling watched or those high-energy times is hard for me. I don’t want to overstate it or give you the wrong impression. I don’t know if I’m explaining that right.\n\nDoctor: When you hear these voices, do they happen even when you’re not feeling particularly down or overly excited?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I mean, there was a time when I wasn’t all keyed up anymore, and my energy had kind of dropped back down. I was sleeping more regular again, so I figured it should’ve stopped, you know? But the voices still kept happening, and I still had that feeling that people were judging my work or kind of tracking what I was doing. It went on for a few weeks like that. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like people are watching you or that your thoughts are being controlled, or have you had any unusual beliefs that others don’t seem to share?\n\nPatient: Um… yes, I guess I have felt that way. I don’t want it to sound like I’m accusing people for no reason, but I got it in my head that the other home-care agency, and even some people from the library, were watching my paintings and my client papers.\n\nIt felt like they were trying to steal some chance that was supposed to be for me. People told me that didn’t really make sense, and I tried to listen, I did… but at the time I still felt like I knew they were doing it. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had strong beliefs about things that others around you didn’t seem to agree with or understand, even when they tried to tell you otherwise?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, I don’t want to make it sound worse than it was, but I did have that. I got really sure that the other home-care agency, and some people from the library too, were watching my paintings and my client papers. Like they were trying to take some opportunity that was supposed to be mine.\n\nPeople told me it didn’t make sense, and I tried to hear them, I really did… but at the time I still felt like I knew it was happening. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had beliefs that something was happening—like people being after your work or watching you—that you held onto even when there was no clear evidence, and these beliefs weren’t just due to feeling down or overly anxious?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s right. I don’t like saying it like I was being unreasonable, but I did hold onto it.\n\nI really believed that the other home-care agency, and some people from the library, were watching my paintings and my client papers… like they were trying to steal some chance that was supposed to be for me. People told me there wasn’t proof and that it didn’t make sense, and I tried to listen, but at the time I still felt like I knew they were doing it.\n\nI wasn’t just sad or nervous when I believed it, if that helps.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt unusually energetic, needed less sleep, or felt like your thoughts were racing — maybe even times where others told you you were acting out of character or too excited?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, I do have times like that. I try to use it in a good way, you know, not just make it sound bad.\n\nI’ll get so much energy that I can’t really sit down. Like I’ll clean my whole kitchen, start three different paintings, crochet half a blanket, and then start rewriting care schedules all in the same night. It feels productive at the time, like I’m finally getting things done… but I guess it is a lot. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: During the times you heard voices or felt people were watching you, did those experiences seem to match how you were feeling emotionally at the time—like if you were feeling down or especially irritable?\n\nPatient: I think… yes, some of it did line up with when I was more keyed up, if that’s the right way to say it.\n\nLike when I was only sleeping a few hours and really pushing these big art and care plans, that’s when I was also hearing the voices in the studio and feeling sure people were watching my work. I wasn’t trying to be dramatic about it — it just felt connected at the time, like everything was moving fast and important.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced hearing voices or feeling like people were watching you when you weren’t feeling particularly down, overly excited, or emotionally elevated?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it was, but there was a stretch after my energy had come down and I was sleeping more normal again where it didn’t fully stop.\n\nThe voices were still there sometimes, and I still had this feeling that people were judging my work or keeping track of what I was doing. It went on for several weeks, I guess. I wasn’t feeling especially up or really down then… at least not in the way I had been before. Does that answer it right?\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a time when you felt completely emotionally stable—neither especially up nor down—where you still experienced hearing voices or feeling like people were watching or judging your work?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that did happen. After my energy kind of dropped back down and I was sleeping more normally, I wasn’t feeling especially high or low, at least not that I could tell.\n\nBut the voices didn’t completely stop, and I still had that feeling like people were judging my work or keeping track of what I was doing. It kept going for several weeks. I know that probably sounds strange… I’m just trying to answer it the right way.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt down most of the day, lost interest in things you usually enjoy, or experienced other symptoms like changes in appetite or trouble concentrating, separate from the times you were feeling very energetic or having these experiences with voices and feeling watched?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can separate it out that clearly. I mean, I’ve had days where I felt tired or kind of low, but I don’t know if it was most of the day or anything like that.\n\nI don’t want to say yes if I’m not remembering it right. I haven’t really paid close attention to appetite or concentration separate from the other stuff. Sorry, is that okay?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period lasting at least two weeks where you felt down most of the day, nearly every day, and lost interest in things you usually enjoy, even when you weren’t having the experiences with voices or feeling watched?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I can honestly say that, no. I’ve had times where I felt low or didn’t feel like doing much, but I’m not sure it was two weeks straight, most of the day, nearly every day… especially separate from everything else.\n\nI don’t want to give you the wrong answer just to be agreeable. I just haven’t tracked it that clearly. Is it okay if I say I’m not sure?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when you felt down or depressed for most of the day, nearly every day for at least two weeks, even when you weren’t hearing voices, feeling watched, or experiencing those high-energy periods?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t think I can say yes to that clearly. I’ve felt down before, but the “most of the day, nearly every day for two weeks” part—I’m not sure.\n\nAnd separating it from the voices or feeling watched or those high-energy times is hard for me. I don’t want to overstate it or give you the wrong impression. I’m sorry… is that the kind of answer you need?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when you felt down or depressed, not because of the voices or feeling watched, and it lasted for most of the day, nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m sorry, I still don’t think I can say that for sure. I’ve had low times, but whether it was most of the day, nearly every day, for two weeks—and not because of the other stuff—I just don’t know.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound worse than it was or give you an answer that isn’t accurate. Is it okay if I say I’m not sure?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

  },

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I’m not sure, doctor. I mean… I’ve had times where I didn’t feel like doing much, or I wasn’t eating or sleeping the best, but I don’t know if that was all together with feeling depressed like that, or if it was for long enough.
